    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n16\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF50.0, F50.1, F50.9, R63.6, E46\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, it’s good to meet you. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Hi. Nice to meet you too. Well, occupational health at my job actually sent me here. They're saying my weight is dangerously low, especially for someone who runs presses and does fabrication all day. \n\nI mean, I didn't really see it that way... I honestly just thought I was getting leaner and more disciplined. But they were pretty insistent about it, so that's why I'm here.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I am. Honestly, if the scale goes up even a little bit, I panic. It just feels like I'm going to lose all the control I've worked so hard for, and I'll just... end up heavy again. So, yes, the thought of putting on weight really scares me.\n\nDoctor: When you look at your body, do you feel like you’re still overweight?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Like, if I catch my reflection in a shop window at work, or just looking in the bathroom mirror... all I can really focus on is my stomach and my thighs. They just look too big to me. And honestly, once I see that, it pretty much ruins how I feel about myself for the rest of the day.\n\nDoctor: Have your periods stopped?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, to be honest. I haven't really been keeping track of them. I guess they've been a little irregular lately, but I haven't paid much attention to whether they've stopped completely.\n\nDoctor: Have you been deliberately eating less in order to lose weight?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. Most days... I mean, I just keep it to coffee before my early shift. Then at lunch, maybe I'll have half a protein bar. And later on, after yoga, just a small bowl of vegetables. I just find that if I eat more than that... it makes the whole day feel completely out of control.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have episodes where you eat a large amount of food in a short time and feel unable to stop?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't do that. I mean, like I said, I pretty much stick to what I planned out for the day. I don't really have times where I just eat a lot all at once or feel like I can't stop. It's actually the opposite... I'm usually just trying to make sure I don't eat too much.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit after eating?\n\nPatient: No, I don't do that either. I don't throw up. I just try to stick to my plan and keep my portions small, like I mentioned. That's really all I do.\n\nDoctor: Do you exercise in a way that feels driven to burn calories or prevent weight gain?\n\nPatient: No, I don't. I mean, I do yoga, like I mentioned before, but it's just a normal class. I don't push myself to do extra workouts or spend hours at the gym just to burn off what I ate. My job at the shop is pretty physical anyway, so I'm on my feet a lot, but I don't exercise specifically to lose weight or anything like that. I just stick to my diet.\n\nDoctor: What is your current height and weight?\n\nPatient: I'm about five foot four. As for my weight, I'm not entirely sure right now. I don't really weigh myself that often. I just focus on how my clothes fit and sticking to my meals.\n\nDoctor: What is the lowest weight you’ve been at your current height?\n\nPatient: I really couldn't tell you an exact number. Like I said, I don't keep track with a scale. I just... I know I've been smaller at times, but I don't know what the actual lowest weight was. I just go by how my clothes feel.\n\nDoctor: Do you know your weight from a recent check at occupational health or a doctor’s visit?\n\nPatient: Well, actually, that's why I'm here. They sent me over from occupational health at the shop. They checked my weight and said it's dangerously low for someone running presses and doing fabrication all day. I didn't get the exact number, but... I honestly just thought I was getting leaner and more disciplined with my diet. I didn't think it was a problem.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a noticeable amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: I don't really think so. I mean, I haven't noticed a big drop recently. My work clothes might be a little looser, but nothing drastic. I see myself every day, so to me, I just look like I'm maintaining the same lean build. So... no, I haven't noticed a lot of weight falling off lately, even if the folks at the clinic thought otherwise.\n\nDoctor: Do you know what your weight was when occupational health checked it?\n\nPatient: Like I mentioned a minute ago, I didn't actually get the exact number. I just stepped on the scale, the nurse looked at it, and then they told me it was too low for me to be working the floor safely. I didn't look at the screen or ask what it said, so... no, I honestly don't know what it was.\n\nDoctor: Do you know your body mass index, or BMI, from that occupational health visit?\n\nPatient: No, I don't know my BMI either. They didn't tell me that number, just like they didn't tell me my weight. They just said it was too low and sent me here.\n\nDoctor: Do you know your weight from any scale at home, work, or a recent medical visit?\n\nPatient: No, I don't. I don't keep a scale at home... I just never really saw the need to have one around. And aside from that health check for work, I haven't been to a regular doctor in a while. So, no, I really don't have any number to give you.\n\nDoctor: Would you be willing to tell me your current weight if we measure it here today?\n\nPatient: I... I'm not sure. I guess if you need to weigh me for your records, that's fine. But I'd really rather not know what the number is. So if you do measure it, I'd prefer you just keep it to yourself and not tell me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Hi. Nice to meet you too. Well, occupational health at my job actually sent me here. They're saying my weight is dangerously low, especially for someone who runs presses and does fabrication all day. \n\nI mean, I didn't really see it that way... I honestly just thought I was getting leaner and more disciplined. But they were pretty insistent about it, so that's why I'm here.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I am. Honestly, if the scale goes up even a little bit, I panic. It just feels like I'm going to lose all the control I've worked so hard for, and I'll just... end up heavy again. So, yes, the thought of putting on weight really scares me.\n\nDoctor: When you look at your body, do you feel like you’re still overweight?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Like, if I catch my reflection in a shop window at work, or just looking in the bathroom mirror... all I can really focus on is my stomach and my thighs. They just look too big to me. And honestly, once I see that, it pretty much ruins how I feel about myself for the rest of the day.\n\nDoctor: Have your periods stopped?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, to be honest. I haven't really been keeping track of them. I guess they've been a little irregular lately, but I haven't paid much attention to whether they've stopped completely.\n\nDoctor: Have you been deliberately eating less in order to lose weight?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. Most days... I mean, I just keep it to coffee before my early shift. Then at lunch, maybe I'll have half a protein bar. And later on, after yoga, just a small bowl of vegetables. I just find that if I eat more than that... it makes the whole day feel completely out of control.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have episodes where you eat a large amount of food in a short time and feel unable to stop?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't do that. I mean, like I said, I pretty much stick to what I planned out for the day. I don't really have times where I just eat a lot all at once or feel like I can't stop. It's actually the opposite... I'm usually just trying to make sure I don't eat too much.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit after eating?\n\nPatient: No, I don't do that either. I don't throw up. I just try to stick to my plan and keep my portions small, like I mentioned. That's really all I do.\n\nDoctor: Do you exercise in a way that feels driven to burn calories or prevent weight gain?\n\nPatient: No, I don't. I mean, I do yoga, like I mentioned before, but it's just a normal class. I don't push myself to do extra workouts or spend hours at the gym just to burn off what I ate. My job at the shop is pretty physical anyway, so I'm on my feet a lot, but I don't exercise specifically to lose weight or anything like that. I just stick to my diet.\n\nDoctor: What is your current height and weight?\n\nPatient: I'm about five foot four. As for my weight, I'm not entirely sure right now. I don't really weigh myself that often. I just focus on how my clothes fit and sticking to my meals.\n\nDoctor: What is the lowest weight you’ve been at your current height?\n\nPatient: I really couldn't tell you an exact number. Like I said, I don't keep track with a scale. I just... I know I've been smaller at times, but I don't know what the actual lowest weight was. I just go by how my clothes feel.\n\nDoctor: Do you know your weight from a recent check at occupational health or a doctor’s visit?\n\nPatient: Well, actually, that's why I'm here. They sent me over from occupational health at the shop. They checked my weight and said it's dangerously low for someone running presses and doing fabrication all day. I didn't get the exact number, but... I honestly just thought I was getting leaner and more disciplined with my diet. I didn't think it was a problem.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a noticeable amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: I don't really think so. I mean, I haven't noticed a big drop recently. My work clothes might be a little looser, but nothing drastic. I see myself every day, so to me, I just look like I'm maintaining the same lean build. So... no, I haven't noticed a lot of weight falling off lately, even if the folks at the clinic thought otherwise.\n\nDoctor: Do you know what your weight was when occupational health checked it?\n\nPatient: Like I mentioned a minute ago, I didn't actually get the exact number. I just stepped on the scale, the nurse looked at it, and then they told me it was too low for me to be working the floor safely. I didn't look at the screen or ask what it said, so... no, I honestly don't know what it was.\n\nDoctor: Do you know your body mass index, or BMI, from that occupational health visit?\n\nPatient: No, I don't know my BMI either. They didn't tell me that number, just like they didn't tell me my weight. They just said it was too low and sent me here.\n\nDoctor: Do you know your weight from any scale at home, work, or a recent medical visit?\n\nPatient: No, I don't. I don't keep a scale at home... I just never really saw the need to have one around. And aside from that health check for work, I haven't been to a regular doctor in a while. So, no, I really don't have any number to give you.\n\nDoctor: Would you be willing to tell me your current weight if we measure it here today?\n\nPatient: I... I'm not sure. I guess if you need to weigh me for your records, that's fine. But I'd really rather not know what the number is. So if you do measure it, I'd prefer you just keep it to yourself and not tell me.\n\nDoctor: Would you allow us to weigh you today?\n\nPatient: Yeah, like I just said, you can weigh me today if you need to. I didn't get the exact number, but... I honestly just thought I was getting leaner and more disciplined with my diet. I didn't think it was a problem.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a noticeable amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: I don't really think so. I mean, I haven't noticed a big drop recently. My work clothes might be a little looser, but nothing drastic. I see myself every day, so to me, I just look like I'm maintaining the same lean build. So... no, I haven't noticed a lot of weight falling off lately, even if the folks at the clinic thought otherwise.\n\nDoctor: Do you know what your weight was when occupational health checked it?\n\nPatient: Like I mentioned a minute ago, I didn't actually get the exact number. I just stepped on the scale, the nurse looked at it, and then they told me it was too low for me to be working the floor safely. I didn't look at the screen or ask what it said, so... no, I honestly don't know what it was.\n\nDoctor: Do you know your body mass index, or BMI, from that occupational health visit?\n\nPatient: No, I don't know my BMI either. They didn't tell me that number, just like they didn't tell me my weight. They just said it was too low and sent me here.\n\nDoctor: Do you know your weight from any scale at home, work, or a recent medical visit?\n\nPatient: No, I don't. I don't keep a scale at home... I just never really saw the need to have one around. And aside from that health check for work, I haven't been to a regular doctor in a while. So, no, I really don't have any number to give you.\n\nDoctor: Would you be willing to tell me your current weight if we measure it here today?\n\nPatient: I... I'm not sure. I guess if you need to weigh me for your records, that's fine. But I'd really rather not know what the number is. So if you do measure it, I'd prefer you just keep it to yourself and not tell me.\n\nDoctor: Would you allow us to weigh you today?\n\nPatient: Yeah, like I just said, you can weigh me today if you need to. Like I said, I asked you not to tell me the number, so I have no idea what the scale said. You'd have to check your own notes for that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I guess they've been a little irregular lately, but I haven't paid much attention to whether they've stopped completely.\n\nDoctor: Have you been deliberately eating less in order to lose weight?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. Most days... I mean, I just keep it to coffee before my early shift. Then at lunch, maybe I'll have half a protein bar. And later on, after yoga, just a small bowl of vegetables. I just find that if I eat more than that... it makes the whole day feel completely out of control.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have episodes where you eat a large amount of food in a short time and feel unable to stop?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't do that. I mean, like I said, I pretty much stick to what I planned out for the day. I don't really have times where I just eat a lot all at once or feel like I can't stop. It's actually the opposite... I'm usually just trying to make sure I don't eat too much.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit after eating?\n\nPatient: No, I don't do that either. I don't throw up. I just try to stick to my plan and keep my portions small, like I mentioned. That's really all I do.\n\nDoctor: Do you exercise in a way that feels driven to burn calories or prevent weight gain?\n\nPatient: No, I don't. I mean, I do yoga, like I mentioned before, but it's just a normal class. I don't push myself to do extra workouts or spend hours at the gym just to burn off what I ate. My job at the shop is pretty physical anyway, so I'm on my feet a lot, but I don't exercise specifically to lose weight or anything like that. I just stick to my diet.\n\nDoctor: What is your current height and weight?\n\nPatient: I'm about five foot four. As for my weight, I'm not entirely sure right now. I don't really weigh myself that often. I just focus on how my clothes fit and sticking to my meals.\n\nDoctor: What is the lowest weight you’ve been at your current height?\n\nPatient: I really couldn't tell you an exact number. Like I said, I don't keep track with a scale. I just... I know I've been smaller at times, but I don't know what the actual lowest weight was. I just go by how my clothes feel.\n\nDoctor: Do you know your weight from a recent check at occupational health or a doctor’s visit?\n\nPatient: Well, actually, that's why I'm here. They sent me over from occupational health at the shop. They checked my weight and said it's dangerously low for someone running presses and doing fabrication all day. I didn't get the exact number, but... I honestly just thought I was getting leaner and more disciplined with my diet. I didn't think it was a problem.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a noticeable amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: I don't really think so. I mean, I haven't noticed a big drop recently. My work clothes might be a little looser, but nothing drastic. I see myself every day, so to me, I just look like I'm maintaining the same lean build. So... no, I haven't noticed a lot of weight falling off lately, even if the folks at the clinic thought otherwise.\n\nDoctor: Do you know what your weight was when occupational health checked it?\n\nPatient: Like I mentioned a minute ago, I didn't actually get the exact number. I just stepped on the scale, the nurse looked at it, and then they told me it was too low for me to be working the floor safely. I didn't look at the screen or ask what it said, so... no, I honestly don't know what it was.\n\nDoctor: Do you know your body mass index, or BMI, from that occupational health visit?\n\nPatient: No, I don't know my BMI either. They didn't tell me that number, just like they didn't tell me my weight. They just said it was too low and sent me here.\n\nDoctor: Do you know your weight from any scale at home, work, or a recent medical visit?\n\nPatient: No, I don't. I don't keep a scale at home... I just never really saw the need to have one around. And aside from that health check for work, I haven't been to a regular doctor in a while. So, no, I really don't have any number to give you.\n\nDoctor: Would you be willing to tell me your current weight if we measure it here today?\n\nPatient: I... I'm not sure. I guess if you need to weigh me for your records, that's fine. But I'd really rather not know what the number is. So if you do measure it, I'd prefer you just keep it to yourself and not tell me.\n\nDoctor: Would you allow us to weigh you today?\n\nPatient: Yeah, like I just said, you can weigh me today if you need to. That's fine. Just... please keep the number to yourself. I really don't want to see it or hear it.\n\nDoctor: What did we find when we weighed you today?\n\nPatient: I don't know. Like I said, I asked you not to tell me the number, so I have no idea what the scale said. You'd have to check your own notes for that.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone recently told you that your weight is dangerously low or far below what is healthy for your height?\n\nPatient: Yeah, they did. Actually, that's why they sent me here from occupational health at my job. They told me my weight is dangerously low for someone who's running presses and doing fabrication all day. I didn't really see it that way, though. I honestly just thought I was getting leaner and, I don't know, more disciplined.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I just never really saw the need to have one around. And aside from that health check for work, I haven't been to a regular doctor in a while. So, no, I really don't have any number to give you.\n\nDoctor: Would you be willing to tell me your current weight if we measure it here today?\n\nPatient: I... I'm not sure. I guess if you need to weigh me for your records, that's fine. But I'd really rather not know what the number is. So if you do measure it, I'd prefer you just keep it to yourself and not tell me.\n\nDoctor: Would you allow us to weigh you today?\n\nPatient: Yeah, like I just said, you can weigh me today if you need to. That's fine. Just... please keep the number to yourself. I really don't want to see it or hear it.\n\nDoctor: What did we find when we weighed you today?\n\nPatient: I don't know. Like I said, I asked you not to tell me the number, so I have no idea what the scale said. You'd have to check your own notes for that.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone recently told you that your weight is dangerously low or far below what is healthy for your height?\n\nPatient: Yeah, they did. Actually, that's why they sent me here from occupational health at my job. They told me my weight is dangerously low for someone who's running presses and doing fabrication all day. I didn't really see it that way, though. I honestly just thought I was getting leaner and, I don't know, more disciplined.\n\nDoctor: Have your periods stopped completely?\n\nPatient: No, they haven't stopped completely.
